### The premise, air-breathing against rocket

**The programme's motivation is a propulsion comparison and the comparison has a literature.** Specific impulse methodology, the range equation and its extension to a payload released in flight, and the propulsion of cruise and tactical missiles are where the premise above is worked out properly.

**33 records.** \[[A General Method for][research_a_general]\] \[[Palaia 2026][research_palaia_2026]\] \[[Batra et al. 2026][research_batra_raute_2026]\] \[[Nguyen et al. 2025][research_nguyen_sonphan_2025]\] \[[Frederick and Petrof 2024][research_frederick_petrof_2024]\] \[[Luca et al. 2024][research_luca_angelo_2024]\] \[[Batra et al. 2023][research_batra_raute_2023]\] \[[Cestino et al. 2023][research_cestino_pisu_2023]\] \[[Harish et al. 2022][research_harish_gladin_2022]\] \[[Staack et al. 2021][research_staack_sobron_2021]\] \[[Chen 2021][research_chen_2021]\] \[[Wroblewski and Ansell 2020][research_wroblewski_ansell_2020]\] \[[de Vries et al. 2020][research_devries_hoogreef_2020]\] \[[Ravishankar and Chakravarthy 2018][research_ravishankar_chakravarthy_2018]\] \[[Randle et al. 2011][research_randle_hall_2011]\] \[[Fleeman 2008][research_fleeman_2008]\] \[[Cavcar 2006][research_cavcar_2006]\] \[[Bardell 2000][research_bardell_2000]\] \[[Schindel 1999][research_schindel_1999]\] \[[Chase et al. 1999][research_chase_mckinney_1999]\] \[[Hoy 1989][research_hoy_1989]\] \[[Stricker 1989][research_stricker_1989]\] \[[High Specific Impulse Propulsion 1987][research_high_specific_1987]\] \[[Turk and Zeiner 1986][research_turk_zeiner_1986]\] \[[Davis 1985][research_davis_1985]\] \[[Davis 1984][research_davis_1984]\] \[[Cruzen 1983][research_cruzen_1983]\] \[[Locke 1981][research_locke_1981]\] \[[Kramer and Buehler 1979][research_kramer_buehler_1979]\] \[[Allen and Byrne 1965][research_allen_byrne_1965]\] \[[Powers 1960][research_powers_1960]\] \[[Deuschle 2025][research_deuschle_2025]\] \[[Tsukanov and Riabkov 2021][research_tsukanov_riabkov_2021]\]

### A missile's energy, its coast and its reach

**This cluster is small, and what it holds is method rather than measurement.** Its records are missile trajectory models and six-degree-of-freedom equations, launch range and launch acceptability region documents, missile drag against flight test, and ballistic coefficient estimation for other flying bodies. **None of its titles names a particular missile's reach**, which is unsurprising for weapons effectiveness and is stated here because a reader should not take a thin record of the numbers for a thin subject.

**20 records.** \[[Zaikang and Defu 2019][research_zaikang_defu_2019]\] \[[Warchulski and Warchulski 2016][research_warchulski_warchulski_2016]\] \[[Herrmann 1996][research_herrmann_1996]\] \[[Holley et al. 1996][research_holley_render_1996]\] \[[Cliff and Kelley 1989][research_cliff_kelley_1989]\] \[[Henton 1971][research_henton_1971]\] \[[Anderson 1970][research_anderson_1970]\] \[[Barnett 1966][research_barnett_1966]\] \[[Barnett 1962][research_barnett_1962]\] \[[Eastman 1993][research_eastman_1993]\] \[[Krieger 1983][research_krieger_1983]\] \[[Krieger 1982][research_krieger_1982]\] \[[Algorithms for Estimating a][research_algorithms_for]\] \[[Біленко and Павлов 2018][research_influence_of_projectile_2018]\] \[[Murthy 2015][research_murthy_2015]\] \[[Li et al. 2022][research_li_shi_2022]\] \[[Common Launch Acceptability Region][research_common_launch]\] \[[Common Launch Acceptability Region][research_common_launch_2]\] \[[Common Launch Acceptability Region][research_common_launch_3]\] \[[Common Launch Acceptability Region][research_common_launch_4]\]

### Attritable and expendable air vehicles as a class

**An attritable vehicle is a cost argument before it is an engineering one**, and the literature accordingly weighs reliability, life-cycle cost and expendability alongside the guidance and identification of small, low-cost vehicles, and says little about their aerodynamics.

**10 records.** \[[Ma et al. 2021][research_ma_docimo_2021]\] \[[Comparing the Cost-Effectiveness 2021][research_comparing_the_2021]\] \[[Colombi et al. 2017][research_colombi_bentz_2017]\] \[[Krings et al. 2013][research_krings_annighofer_2013]\] \[[Erdman and Mitchum 2013][research_erdman_mitchum_2013]\] \[[Dorobantu et al. 2013][research_dorobantu_murch_2013]\] \[[Kahn and Edwards 2012][research_kahn_edwards_2012]\] \[[Bateman et al. 2007][research_bateman_nelson_2007]\] \[[Case Study 2007][research_case_study_2007]\] \[[Hawkins 1989][research_hawkins_1989]\]

### Recovery of a test article

**A recovery system on a vehicle meant to be expended is a test-programme artefact**, and the parachute and decelerator literature is where its sizing lives.

**21 records.** \[[Drag and Performance Characteristics][research_drag_and]\] \[[To n.d.][research_to_2024]\] \[[Dinh n.d.][research_dinh_2024]\] \[[Drynan n.d.][research_drynan_2024]\] \[[Pepermans et al. 2018][research_pepermans_rozemeijer_2018]\] \[[Clark et al. 2015][research_clark_gallon_2015]\] \[[Gallon and Witkowski 2015][research_gallon_witkowski_2015]\] \[[Cook 2013][research_cook_2013]\] \[[Guidotti et al. 2012][research_guidotti_richiello_2012]\] \[[Wolf and Fallon 2011][research_wolf_fallon_2011]\] \[[Morris and Olson 2011][research_morris_olson_2011]\] \[[Witkowski 1999][research_witkowski_1999]\] \[[Behr 1989][research_behr_1989]\] \[[Pepper 1984][research_pepper_1984]\] \[[Pepper 1980][research_pepper_1980]\] \[[Pepper 1979][research_pepperjr_1979]\] \[[Pepper 1977][research_pepper_1977]\] \[[Whalley 1973][research_whalley_1973]\] \[[Flatau and Miller 1970][research_flatau_miller_1970]\] \[[Babish 1966][research_babishiii_1966]\] \[[Ericksen et al. 1966][research_ericksen_guiteras_1966]\]

### Demonstrator practice

**This cluster is nearly empty, and for a reason of its own.** Writing about how to run a demonstrator programme is mostly institutional rather than indexed, so the indexed literature holds almost none of it. The practice is real and the literature about the practice is thin.

**2 records.** \[[Artificial Intelligence Techniques for][research_artificial_intelligence]\] \[[Flight Test Programme 1970][research_flight_test_1970]\]

## Where the Framing Breaks Down

**Treating this aircraft through a store mass fraction is the right frame, yet it misleads in at
least seven ways, each named here so that a reader need not find it.**

### The Keystone Parameter Cannot Be Evaluated

**$\mu$ is unpublished because the vehicle's mass is unpublished**, so every figure in this article
that depends on it is a sweep rather than a measurement. The sweep's brackets are argued from the
engine's provenance and that argument is an inference. **A reader who takes
the 17.94 percent of the $\mu_v$ display under The Research Question as this aircraft's store mass
fraction is taking the middle of a sweep as a fact**, and this article's only defence against that is to keep saying so.

### A Static Margin Band Is a Judgement and the Bound Inherits It

The admissible store arm was derived from a requirement that the static margin stay inside a band.
**The band is not derived from anything here.** A vehicle with a full-authority digital control
system and a deliberately relaxed static margin may tolerate a far larger excursion than a
conventionally stable aeroplane, and some combat aircraft are flown statically unstable on purpose.
**The bound is therefore conditional on a stability philosophy this article does not know.**

### The Release Is Treated as Instantaneous

**Every relation above compares a before state with an after state.** A real release has a duration,
during which the store is partly supported, partly free, and aerodynamically interfering with the
vehicle it is leaving. **The difficulty lives in the transient**, the
part that closed-form mass arithmetic cannot reach. This article's mathematics describes the endpoints
of the event the programme exists to study.

### The Plume Is Not Treated At All

**If the missile's motor ignites within a few body lengths of the vehicle, the vehicle is in a rocket
exhaust.** Pressure, temperature and particulates on a structure sized for cruise are a design
driver, and nothing in this article addresses them. Whether the missile is ejected and coasts clear
before ignition, or fires while attached, is not established by any document read here, and the two
cases are not close to each other in difficulty.

### The Drag Model Behind the Reach Numbers Is One Coefficient

**The coast derivation assumes a constant drag coefficient and a constant density.** A real missile
flies through a changing atmosphere at a changing Mach number with a drag coefficient that varies
considerably across the transonic region, and it manoeuvres, which costs further energy. **The
calibration against published reach figures is the reason to trust the comparison** between the
carrier and the missile, but gives no reason to trust any single reach figure as a prediction.

### The Comparison Flatters the Carrier by Ignoring What It Costs to Carry

**The carrier must itself be carried.** A launch aircraft giving up a station or a bay volume to a
vehicle of this size gives up the stores it would otherwise have carried there, and the reach gain
should properly be computed per unit of launch-aircraft capacity rather than per missile. **This
article does not compute that**, because the vehicle's volume and the exchange ratio against
conventional stores are both unpublished. **The omission is in the optimistic direction and is the
most important of the seven.**

### The Mission Statement Under the Frame Is Not Official

**The register's mission sentence is the compiler's**, as the second section established. The budget
books describe a programme and not an aeroplane. **So the frame is built on an official programme
objective and an unofficial aircraft description**, and a reader should hold the aircraft-level
claims more loosely than the programme-level ones.

## The Source Base

**The research survey holds 417 records across 11 clusters, cited alongside 26 primary sources read
directly.** Its records come from NASA's Technical Reports Server, the Defense Technical Information
Center and the journal, conference and standards registries that Crossref indexes. **365 of the 417
carry a year and 349 of those are dated 2025 or earlier**, with a median year of 2009 and a range
from 1948 to 2026, and the count is reported beside the fraction because adding contemporary work
lowers the fraction while leaving the count unchanged.

### The Reports Server Is the Wrong Server for This Subject

**Only 8 of the 417 cited records carry an address on NASA's Technical Reports Server, against 58
that carry a Defense Technical Information Center DOI.**

**That is not a thin literature, it is a subject living somewhere else.** Store separation is a
weapons integration discipline, and weapons integration is a defence activity and not a civil
aeronautics one. **An account of the literature drawn from the civil server alone would describe it
as thin when it is large.**

### What This Article Read in Full

**26 sources were read directly rather than surveyed by title.** The designation register and its
front matter. All seven budget justification books, for the programme entry, the funding
columns, the plans lists and the programme element migration sentences. The sponsoring agency's
programme page and its announcement. The contractor's announcement. The federal award record, for
five awards and their contract data. Three defence journalism accounts. The compiler's page on the
designations of military aero-engines. An index record of the compatibility handbook, for its
title, its date and the standard it supersedes. And the reference
pages for the cruise missile that shares this aircraft's engine, the missile the programme says it
will carry, and the aircraft that will launch it. And the register's page as archived on three dates,
for the note under Statements in This Article That Postdate Its Own Date.

**Every load-bearing anchor but the engine's thrust rests on a primary document.** The launch aircraft's weights
come from the service's own fact sheet, read from a public archive snapshot because the service's
site refuses every client, and quoted by its pound figures because the sheet's own metric conversion
of its take-off weight is 291 kilograms low. The cruise missile's mass and the word turbofan come
from the manufacturer's own datasheet. The atmosphere subsection's constants carry the 1976
standard's own tables, read as page images from an image-only scan. The missile's dimensions and its
baseline launch weight carry the service's fact sheet, read from a public archive snapshot. **The
engine's thrust figure is encyclopedia-sourced because neither manufacturer document states it, and
the modelling mass for the missile is the encyclopedia's heavy-variant figure by a stated
choice**, the service sheet giving the baseline variant. The thrust is the weakest link in
the chain, anchoring as it does the thrust-to-weight table, the climb gradients and the mass
sweep's brackets.

**And a small finding about the fact sheets themselves.** Both of the service's sheets convert
their own pound figures to kilograms incorrectly, the aircraft's take-off weight printed 291
kilograms low and the missile's launch weight 1.2 kilograms low. **This article therefore quotes
both documents by their pound figures and converts at the definition of the pound**, so that
neither discrepancy becomes this article's own.

**And two documents are cited as addresses rather than as readings, which are different things
and are kept apart.** The broad agency announcement HR001120S0037, named by four awards, could
not be retrieved from the federal opportunity portal, the public web archive or the sponsoring
agency's own site, and the discipline's governing compatibility handbook sits behind the standards
repository's login, so only its index record was read. **Each is cited by its nominal address, the portal page and the repository's
public search page, neither is counted among the 26 sources read directly, and no claim
in this article rests on either document's content.**

### The Definition of Primary

**Primacy is a property of the document, and this survey derives it from the address.** A record is
a report primary when its address is on NASA's Technical Reports Server, the Defense Technical
Information Center or the Department of Energy's Office of Scientific and Technical Information, or
when its digital object identifier, or DOI, is one of their report DOIs. **67 of the 417, or 16.1
percent, are report primaries by that rule.** **Store separation is an aerospace engineering
discipline published mainly through one professional society's conferences and journals**, and 222
of the 417, or 53.2 percent, carry a DOI under the prefix 10.2514 of the American Institute of
Aeronautics and Astronautics, so most of the discipline's first-hand work sits in the article
registries and not on the government report servers. **The rule therefore understates how much of
the survey is first-hand**, since a conference paper reporting a wind tunnel or flight test is a
primary account of that test whatever its address.

### Thin Subjects

**Six subjects that this article's derivations depend on have thin literatures.** Each count below
is the number of research records in this survey whose title carries at least one of the listed
terms, with a hyphen read as a space. Where a row joins two groups of terms with the word together,
a title must carry a term from each group.

| Subject | Title terms counted | Records |
|---|---|---:|
| a store released from an unmanned carrier | uav, uavs, unmanned, ucav, ucavs, drone, drones together with store, stores, weapon, weapons, missile, missiles, release, separation, drop | 9 |
| the ejector's force and stroke | ejector, ejection | 15 |
| the plume of a missile fired near its carrier | plume, plumes | 1 |
| a missile's reach | launch range, maximum range, reach, launch acceptability, engagement zone, engagement zones | 6 |
| the change of inertia at release | inertia, inertias, moment of inertia, moments of inertia | 1 |
| the retrim after release | trim, trims, trimmed, trimming, retrim, retrimming | 0 |

**The literature on unmanned carriers studies their bays more than their releases.** Of the 9
titles, 4 name a weapon bay, and 3 concern something released from the unmanned vehicle itself, of
which 1 names a weapon. The keystone, a small unmanned vehicle releasing a store of large mass
fraction, therefore rests on this article's own arithmetic rather than on a published analysis.

**The ejector's force has become a modelling subject only recently.** Of the 15 titles, 4 model the
ejector's force or the acceleration it imparts, and all 4 date from 2022 or later. The recoil and
reduced-mass relations under The Ejector Pushes Back are classical mechanics and do not depend on
those models, but the cartridge impulse they take as an input does.

**The plume appears once**, in a simulation of missile plumes for aircraft and store compatibility
assessments, which is consistent with this article's own decision to leave the plume out of scope.

**The reach literature is method without a missile.** None of the 6 titles names a particular
missile, so the reach figures under The Premise rest on the coast model calibrated against the
encyclopedia's published range figures.

**Inertia and trim are named almost nowhere.** One title names inertia, and it concerns the
time-varying inertias of a variable-sweep morphing aircraft rather than a release. No title names
trim, so the two inertia corrections and the retrim demand derived above stand on the relations
shown and not on a measured case.

### What the Survey Admits

**A record is admitted only when a person reading its title finds it on this article's subject**,
and twin deposits of one document under one title are merged so that each document is cited once.
The subject is read as the eleven literatures the clusters name, each of which a section of this
article uses, from the release of a carried store and its carriage, mechanism and effect on the
carrier, through air launch, folding surfaces and recovery, to the propulsion premise, a missile's
reach, attritable vehicles and demonstrator practice.

**Several words this subject depends on also name something else, and a record that shares only such
a word is excluded.** Breguet names a watchmaker and an aircraft maker as well as the range equation.
Store also names a shop, a memory in cognitive psychology and the storey of a building, and centre of
gravity also names a concept in economic geography. Trajectory also describes strategic and ballistic
missile flight from burnout to impact and boost-glide flight, which are missile flight rather than
store release, and it describes missile guidance, autopilot design, evasion and terminal trajectory
optimisation, which are excluded for the same reason. Specific impulse also belongs to spacecraft
electric and laser propulsion, and air-breathing propulsion also names combustion, thermal
management and teaching, which are excluded because the premise is a comparison and not a survey of
engines. Air-launched also describes oceanographic instruments dropped from aircraft, loading also
describes the ground equipment that loads an aircraft gun, ejection also names a pilot's seat and a
hydrocyclone, and time-varying also names classes of systems with delay, periodicity or special
structure, which are excluded because a release is a step change. Correction notices, withdrawal
notices and withdrawn papers are excluded as well, because a survey counts research records and
those are editorial events rather than works.

**Separation is the hardest of these words, because its largest other use is aeronautical.**
Boundary-layer separation, flow separation, leading-edge separation and turbulent separation control
are the largest aeronautical users of the word separation. **They are aerodynamics**, so no test of
whether a title is aeronautical tells them apart from store separation. **A title is therefore read
as on this subject only when it names a thing that is carried and released**, and the word separation
standing alone admits nothing.

**Some records of doubtful relevance are kept.** Air-launched meteorological sondes and balloon
systems, ballistic coefficient estimation for re-entry bodies and non-lethal projectiles, mass
properties reports for an intercontinental ballistic missile, air-breathing and rocket propulsion
compared for trans-atmospheric and space transport vehicles, propellant chemistry for an
air-launched missile, cooperative air combat tactics read through weapon engagement zones, and a
fixed-wing unmanned aircraft delivering a payload each share either the carried and released
object or the physics of a release or a coast with this subject, so each is kept and a reader may
weigh it accordingly.

**Every research title has been read for relevance, so the off-topic share that remains is a matter
of reading judgement.** A reading of titles cannot show that a work is relevant in substance, so
this is a statement about titles only.

## Epistemic State

**Historical fact, from documents read for this article.**

The X-68A designation was allocated on 20 August 2025 to General Atomics, with the engine, sponsor
and description cells as quoted, and the description carries a span-level officiality mark whose
effect is that the name is official Department wording and the mission sentence is not. The
programme is LongShot, sponsored by the Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency. Five
awards in the federal award record carry it, totalling 148,305,710.65 dollars, with the parties,
dates, amounts, offer counts and product codes as tabulated. Seven budget justification books
carry a programme entry, the funding figures and restatements are as tabulated, and the programme
moved from programme element 0603286E project AIR-01 to programme element 0603468E project ACX-01
beginning in fiscal year 2026 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum][ref_mds_addendum]\] \[[USAspending federal award record][ref_usaspending]\]
\[[DARPA PB2021 justification][ref_darpa_pb2021]\] \[[DARPA PB2027 justification][ref_darpa_pb2027]\].

**Engineering analysis, derived here from published anchors and stated assumptions.**

Every relation in the sizing and premise sections is derived here and not cited, and each is a
closed-form consequence of mass, geometry and the two propulsion relations. **None of it is
measured from this aircraft**, because no mass, dimension or aerodynamic coefficient of this
aircraft is published. The ballistic decay model is calibrated against the published reach of the
missile family the programme names, which is the only external check available to it.

**Where each load-bearing anchor rests.** The launch aircraft's weights are the service fact
sheet's pound figures exactly converted. The cruise missile's mass and its engine's type are the
manufacturer's datasheet. The atmosphere constants are the 1976 standard's adopted tables. The carried missile's
dimensions and baseline weight are the service's fact sheet, with the modelling mass the
encyclopedia's heavy-variant figure by the stated choice. **The engine's thrust is an
encyclopedia's figure and nothing stronger**, and every result standing on it, the
thrust-to-weight table, the climb gradients and the mass sweep's brackets, inherits that
weakness.
The ratios and closed forms do not, since they are arithmetic whatever the inputs. **Two
reference definitions are addresses and not readings**, the solicitation's portal page and the
standards repository's search page for the compatibility handbook, each marked as such where
cited, and neither document's content informs any claim here.

**Inference, labelled as such.**

That the vehicle's mass lies in the swept range of 1,000 to 3,000
kilograms is an inference from the engine's provenance and is not stated anywhere. That the concept
change the budget books record was a change in the aircraft rather than in the prose is an
inference, supported by the award record's preserved wording and by the engine being single mode.
That the press figure for the demonstration award differs from the record's because one is an
initial obligation and the other a current total is a plausible explanation this article has not
confirmed in any document.

**What the record does not settle.**

Whether `WJ38-15` parses as a standard military engine designation, and therefore whether the
register's turbojet or the manufacturer designation's turbofan is correct. Which of the two named
individuals is currently the programme manager. Whether the missile fires while attached or coasts
clear before ignition, which is the difference between two very different structural problems.
Whether the in-flight missile separation demonstration that PB2024, PB2025 and PB2026 plan, and
PB2027 does not name, was deferred, reorganised or removed, and in which year the ejection of a
captive sub-munition that the announcement of February 2026 promises is to be flown. **And the keystone itself, since no vehicle of this class has
released a store of this mass fraction in flight in any record read here.**

### Statements in This Article That Postdate Its Own Date

**This article is dated 13 December 2025 and a number of its statements are later.** The survey
is written from current knowledge and this section marks where, and this article has more such
statements than most because its subject became public after its date.

**The designation was allocated 115 days before this article's date and
announced 66 days after it**, on 17 February 2026. So at the article's date
the designation had been allocated and had not been publicly announced.

**A note on the register.** The register row this article reads was not public at this article's date either. The register's page as archived on 10 December 2025 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 10 December 2025][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2025_12]\] and on 15 January 2026 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 15 January 2026][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2026_01]\] ends its research rows at the X-66A, and the page as archived on 1 February 2026 \[[Parsch, DOD 4120.15-L Addendum, as archived on 1 February 2026][ref_mds_addendum_wb_2026_02]\] carries the X-68A row, so the row first appeared between 15 January and 1 February 2026, 148 to 165 days after the allocation it records. **Every reading of the row in this article is therefore a reading of a page later than the article's date**, like the announcement.

The following are outside the date and are used anyway, each marked where it appears.

- The announcement of 17 February 2026 by the sponsoring agency and the contractor's response.
- The full-scale wind tunnel, parachute recovery and weapons-release milestones those announcements
  report.
- The F-15 as the launch aircraft for the first flight tests, the statement that flight testing
  could begin as early as the end of 2026, and the statement that those tests will demonstrate the
  safe ejection of a captive sub-munition.
- The name of the programme manager quoted in that announcement.
- **April 2026's budget justification book**, which is the seventh and the only one
  outside the date \[[DARPA PB2027 justification][ref_darpa_pb2027]\]. Its funding figures for fiscal years 2025 through 2027 and its plans lists are
  used, and every table marks which column came from it.
- The statement that parachute recovery is intended for test and training and not combat.
- The two defence journalism accounts of 2026 \[[The War Zone on the X-68A][ref_twz_x68a]\]
  \[[Air and Space Forces on the X-68A][ref_asf_x68a]\].
- **The federal award record, which was read in October 2026.** The amounts stated are the
  record's current totals and reflect contract modifications through May 2026, so the Phase 3
  figure in particular may be larger than the figure that stood at this article's
  date \[[USAspending federal award record][ref_usaspending]\].
- The register row itself, as the note above explains.
- The research literature, which is drawn from current indexes and includes records dated to
  2026.

**And two sources sit inside the date by their content and outside it by their reading.** The two
service fact sheets were read from archive snapshots taken on 1 and 4 December 2025, both inside
this article's date, so the pages cited are the pages as they stood at the time. The readings
themselves, like every reading here, happened later.

## Out of Scope

**The engagement, the subject most writing about this programme chooses.** Whether a
forward-launched missile carrier changes air combat, what an adversary does about it, and how it
interacts with the collaborative combat aircraft programmes are questions about operations rather
than about an aeroplane, and this article is about an aeroplane.

**The guidance and the datalink.** A vehicle launching a missile at a target it did not find needs
targeting data from somewhere, and the architecture that supplies it is a separate subject with its
own literature. The PB2025 book plans weapon integration and ground testing as a separate line
\[[DARPA PB2025 justification][ref_darpa_pb2025]\], and that integration is not treated here.

**The missile itself.** The programme plans to use existing missiles and this article takes one
of them as a mass, a length and a published reach. Its seeker, its fuze, its warhead and its
control system are out of scope.

**The plume and the structural response to it**, named in the framing section as a gap rather than
dismissed. It would need a thermal and pressure analysis this article does not attempt.

**Aeroelasticity.** Store-induced flutter appears in the literature cluster because it is part of
the discipline, and this article's mathematics is rigid-body throughout. A folding surface on a
slender vehicle releasing a heavy store is an aeroelastic problem and is not treated as one here.

**The cost of the vehicle.** The attritable literature is cited as a body and the vehicle's unit
cost is not public, so the economics that would decide whether this is bought are absent.

**The other designations in this article's neighbourhood**, which have their own articles in this
series.

## Conclusion

**The X-68A is an aeroplane whose every official word is bookkeeping, and whose purpose has no
official word but its name.** The register that names it marks its mission sentence as the
compiler's, and no other research allocation in that register is marked that way. The budget justification books describe the
programme at length, though not the aircraft. **No public Department
document read for this article says what the X-68A is.**

**What those books do say, four times running, is what the programme is for.** It exists to address
the stability and control challenges of launching air-to-air missiles from a relatively small
unmanned vehicle, and that sentence is the research question. **The reach benefit that every
account of the programme leads with is the motivation, and the motivation is arithmetic.** A
missile's reach is logarithmic in its speed ratio, so doubling it with a bigger motor would take the
propellant fraction from about 0.402 to about 0.746 and leave nothing for a
warhead. The same distance costs an air-breathing carrier a fuel fraction near 0.013.
**Separating the transit from the endgame is not an optimisation but the only available move**, and
that is why the programme exists.

**The research question is a mass ratio.** A fighter releasing one of these missiles sheds about
0.601 percent of itself. A vehicle of this class releasing two sheds something near
17.94 percent, a factor of about 30. **Everything follows from that one
number**, amplified by the factor that grows faster than it does. The centre of gravity moves, the
static margin can be spent in a single event, the trim demand lands hardest where control power is
scarcest, the inertia changes by two corrections rather than one, an asymmetric release loads the
lateral control, and the ejector pushes the launcher as hard as it pushes the store.

**The budget books also show the programme becoming this aeroplane.** It began as a weapon system
with multi-mode propulsion, and in a window of 11 months between two books it
became an air-breathing vehicle carrying existing missiles. **The award record corroborates that
independently**, holding the original concept verbatim in a contract description dated January 2021,
and the single-mode cruise missile engine in the register's cell is the physical trace of the same
change.

**And the plans tell one more thing that no single book reveals.** The in-flight release of a
missile from the vehicle, which is the keystone as a demonstration, is planned by three consecutive
books for three different years, is named as the missile's separation only in the first of them,
is joined by a ground drop of a substitute mass in the third, and is not named at all in the first
book after this article's date, which plans the vehicle's release for FY2026 and FY2027 while the
announcement of two months earlier promises the ejection of a captive sub-munition without a year.
**A plans list is a summary and
not a schedule, so this is an observation about documents rather than a claim about engineering.**
It is recorded because the thing the programme states it exists to retire slipped two fiscal years
like any other milestone and then lost its year in the published plans, and a reader of any one book
would not see it.

**At this article's date nothing had flown.** The designation was 115 days
old and unannounced, the flight test campaign had not begun, and captive carry had
been promised by three consecutive books for three different years. **The question
remains open, and a good one**, because the answer transfers to every vehicle that tries
to carry a weapon it is barely larger than.
